The mock is rendered: on the left, your real exported frame at 3 s; on the right, Bold with colours measured from the recording. My honest read: the "after" reads as one designed piece, while the "before" reads as separate pieces on a red that has nothing to do with your video.

One weakness shows in the mock itself: at that size, word-by-word captions run to four lines. When I build, Bold would show fewer words at a time.

Here's the proposal; nothing is built.

**The four presets**

| | **Clean** | **Bold** | **Cinematic** | **Playful** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Colour look** (on the picture, via the existing grade) | Neutral, slightly brighter | Contrast +15%, saturation +20% | Contrast +10%, saturation −15%, cool | Saturation +25%, warm |
| **Designed background** | Soft light-to-dark gradient tinted by the video's colour; thin bar; no wave | Deep video colour to its neighbour hue; thick accent bar; no wave | Near-black with a faint video-colour glow; thin bar; no wave | Bright video colour to its complementary; moving shapes; wave dots kept |
| **Captions** | Lines, white on a translucent band, sans, ×1.1 | Word by word, heavy sans ×1.8, the word being said on an accent chip | Lines, serif ×1.2, no band, soft shadow | Highlight mode, sans ×1.4, accent band behind the word |
| **Caption and accent colours** | From the video, contrast ≥ 4.5:1 | From the video, ≥ 4.5:1 | From the video, muted, ≥ 4.5:1 | From the video, brightened, ≥ 4.5:1 |
| **Transition** (one kind per video, eased) | Dissolve 0.4 s | Slide 0.3 s (already eased) | Fade through black 0.8 s | Zoom cut |
| **Title animation** | Fade in | Slide in, accent chip | Slow fade, word by word | Pop |
| **Zoom on key words** | ×1.08 | ×1.25 | ×1.12, slower push | ×1.35 |
| **Pacing** (visual only) | Titles hold 3 s, key lines 3–4 s | Quick: titles 2 s, short transitions | Slow: longer holds and transitions | Quick |
| **Music mood** (defined, unused) | Calm | Upbeat | Cinematic | Upbeat, fun |
| **Suits** | Screen demos, talking heads, professional | Promos and ads, reels | Storytelling, talking heads, brand promos | Reels, funny clips, casual talking heads |

**My recommendations on the open points:**
1. **The four existing Make it styles: map, don't replace.** reel, professional, funny and ad stay as what the video is, guiding Gemini's cutting and the card's heading ("An ad, about…"). They lose all look settings, which move into the presets. Left to You decide, each style has a default: professional → Clean, reel → Bold, ad → Bold, funny → Playful. Cinematic is only picked when asked.
2. **Picking the preset:**
   - **By code:** the plan picks it with no new Gemini field, in this order: your click, then a style word in your request or a follow-up, then the style's default.
   - **Card line:** "As you asked: Bold look", or "Chosen for you: Bold look, it suits an ad".
   - **Follow-up switch:** "more cinematic" switches it, and the latest ask wins.
3. **The style question:** "Which look?" Clean · Bold · Cinematic · Playful · You decide.
   - **Asked** only when the request names no style. The style words, whole words only: clean/minimal/simple, bold/punchy, cinematic/filmic/moody/dramatic, playful/fun/funny.
   - **Screen size:** with what it is, the ad's shape, length, style, and tone plus call to action, that's at most 5 questions.
4. **Colour from the video:**
   - **Source:** the pictures Make it already captures, every 2 s, at no cost.
   - **Usable colour:** pixels with saturation ≥ 0.35 and lightness between 0.15 and 0.85, bucketed by hue.
   - **Fallback:** if under 1% of pixels qualify, the preset's own palette.
   - **Readable text:** text colours are lightened or darkened until they reach at least 4.5:1 against what's behind them (the WCAG AA level).
5. **Easing:** today slides ease, but fades and dissolves are linear. I'd add an easing flag that only transitions the plan makes carry. Hand-made transitions in the editor, and their tests, stay exactly as they are.
6. **Zoom on key words:** zooms only on sentences that hold a key line. Each starts at the first quoted word and holds to the end of the sentence, at the preset's strength. Screen recordings and designed frames still never zoom (that rule doesn't change).
7. **Pacing is visual only:** title and key-line hold times, transition length and zoom speed, never the cuts. That way your saved real runs keep the same clips, lengths, shape, treatment and labels; only the look changes.
8. **Existing tests:** presets set the look, so Gemini's own caption and transition picks and the fixed style colours no longer decide it. Many existing assertions about the look will change, including the #38 replay fixture, whose look fields change in every scenario. Per your rule, I'll build, then bring you every changed expectation side by side before changing any. For the fixture, I'll prove field by field that only look fields moved.

The mock is at `scratchpad\style-mock\bold-before-after.png`. I can copy it to your Desktop if you'd like it there.

Do you confirm the four presets as tabled, and recommendations 1 to 8? I recommend them all as shown. Or tell me which row or point to change.

The Bold fix is pushed to PR #41 as `97fd7fd`. CI hasn't finished yet: my one API check found the Claude review passed and the main CI run still running. As you asked, no watcher is left running and nothing is merged. Tell me when to check again and I'll report once every check has finished. The previous commit, `11d3fed`, was fully green.

**1. Bold's band.**
- **What it is now:** on designed frames, Bold's captions and title sit on a solid, saturated block with white text at 4.5:1 or more. It keeps the accent's hue unless that's within 60° of the background's hues; then it takes the complement of the background's hue.
- **Other looks:** their bands stay see-through. The solid block needed one small optional field on text styles.
- **The title:** I gave Bold's designed title the same block, since it had the same pale band. Say if you want that limited to captions.
- **One removal:** the accent lightening I added earlier in this PR only ever served Bold's old band. A sweep of 1,080 video colours showed it never changed Playful, so it was dead code with no test.

**2. One word at a time.**
- **The blank 12 s Bold frame wasn't a gap inside a line.** It's a pause of over 0.7 s between lines, and every look, and main, is blank at that moment.
- **The code already held each word until the next starts** within a line.
- **I added a test that pins it:** every moment of every spoken line shows a word, and only the pause between lines is blank.

**3. Does Bold look bold now? Yes.**
- **Screen recording:** a magenta block with white text on the green background, at both 9:16 and 16:9. It's loud, and on a green video it's a strong complementary clash. That reads as Bold, though some may find it garish.
- **Talking head:** Bold's captions sit over the picture on the black bars, so the block doesn't appear there and it's unchanged.
- **Weak spot:** the progress bar is still pale green, not magenta.

**Do any looks look too similar on the screen recording?** Clean and Cinematic do. Both are dark green, and they differ mainly in Cinematic's serif, darker grade and missing caption band. Bold (magenta block) and Playful (green-to-purple gradient, mint highlight, wave) stand apart. Because every colour comes from the video, all four share the same green base.

**4. Tests.**
- New tests cover each rule: solid, white, saturated, at least 4.5:1, at least 60° from the background, keep-or-complement, and no blank inside a spoken line.
- **All 24 rules turn a named test red when broken on purpose** (17 earlier plus 7 new).
- **Unit tests:** 686 (685 pass, 1 Windows-only skip).
- **Replay fixture:** only 68 look fields changed since the last push. The proof against main is unchanged.

CONTEXT
PR #41 is open (look presets). Bold's designed-frame caption band now
shifts to the complement of the background's hue, which gives magenta on
my green screen recording. I find that bad. What looked good to me was
a green or blue-green background with BLACK fill behind the text, and
text that was white and green. The text colour matters most; the fill
is optional.

GOAL

**What changed:**
- **A webcam bubble over a screen is now a screen.** It counts as one when Gemini says one person with a corner card, and the face sits in a corner of the picture in at least half the pictures with a face. CONTEXT
Retake is my Next.js 16 video editor (React 19, Prisma, local
Supabase, Gemini and Deepgram for AI, browser-side export with
MediaRecorder, projects stored in IndexedDB). It has changed a lot
since it was last reviewed. I want the whole repository on main as it
is today reviewed again, from scratch, as if you had never seen it.
Don't use any earlier score or review as a starting point. Read the
code, not the notes: PROGRESS_NOTES.md and ARCHITECTURE.md describe
intentions, so check every claim in them against the code before you
count it in the repo's favour.

GOAL
Give me an honest, independent architecture review of the entire
codebase. I want to know what a strict reviewer would deduct marks for,
what is a sound trade-off, and whether the app could be deployed for
real users without surprises in cost, security or reliability. Success
is a score out of 15 with reasons, plus a ranked list I can act on,
with evidence for every item.

WHAT TO DO
Read-only. Don't change any code, make any commit or call any paid API.
1. Review the whole structure: all folders, module boundaries, how
   responsibilities are split, the biggest files and functions, and
   where one file does too much.
2. Review the tech choices against what the app needs: Next.js server
   actions, Gemini plus Deepgram, browser-side export, IndexedDB
   project storage, local Supabase auth, Prisma. For each, say whether
   it fits and what the trade-off is.
3. Review security and cost on every paid server action: authentication
   inside the action, rate limits, quota checks before the provider
   call, request-size and timeout limits, and what happens if the auth
   service is down. Also review the public contact form, the sign-in
   redirect, secrets handling and the environment checks.
4. Review reliability: export interruption and long videos, storage
   failures and quota, media identity, error handling, and the tests and
   CI gates (including what the tests would NOT catch).
5. Deployment readiness: if this ran for real users, what would break,
   what would cost money unexpectedly, and what is missing (logging,
   monitoring, limits, environments, migrations on a hosted database)?
   Don't deploy anything or use hosted services.
6. Check that documentation and comments match the code, and say where
   they don't.
7. Score the architecture out of 15. Show how you got the number: what
   each area contributed and what cost marks. Then say how a different
   strict reviewer might score it, higher and lower, and why.

DONE MEANS
- A score out of 15 with a short breakdown by area.
- A ranked list of findings. Each has: the file and line, what is
  wrong, why a reviewer would care, the smallest fix, a size (small,
  medium, large) and a confidence (sure or needs checking).
- A separate list of things that are fine, or deliberate trade-offs
  that just need documenting.
- A short answer to "could this be deployed for real users": yes, no or
  with what changes.
- Your recommendation: the three things to do first, and what to leave.

HOW TO VERIFY
Show the evidence for each finding: the file, the line, the exact code,
or the exact thing that is missing. If you didn't check something, say
so instead of guessing.

RULES
- Don't assume anything. If something is unclear, ask me one question at
  a time with your recommended answer. Don't ask about things you can
  check yourself.
- Be adversarial. You wrote much of this code, so don't defend it.
  Report weaknesses plainly, including the awkward ones, and don't round
  a score up for effort.
- No code changes, no commits, no real API calls, no hosted services.
  Never touch design/. Don't print any key values.
- If a step fails or memory runs out, tell me what happened and stop.

WHEN DONE
Tell me in a few sentences what matters most and what you recommend I do
first.

# Architecture review: Retake, `main` at `f532954`

I reviewed it from scratch against a clean copy of `origin/main`, read-only: no code changes, no commits, no AI calls. I checked documentation claims against the code before counting them. Line numbers refer to `main`.

## Score: 8 / 15

| Area (max 3 each) | Score | What earned marks | What cost marks |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Structure and modularity** | 1.5 | Clear folders by concern (`editor/model`, `overlays`, `audio`, `export`, `projects`); planner split into named, separately tested steps; one place for AI calls | `VideoEditor` is a single 2,560-line component; `make-plan.ts` is 2,058 lines; about 1,640 lines of unused template scaffolding |
| **Tech fit** | 2 | Gemini + Deepgram behind one wrapper; browser-side editing and export (no media on servers, no transcoding bill); Prisma + Postgres for small, well-defined server data | Server actions carry the audio and pictures (up to about 13 MB) in one request, which doesn't fit the documented host |
| **Security and cost** | 1.5 | Every paid action: sign-in check inside the action, failing closed; input validated before spending; per-user daily allowance taken atomically before the call; timeouts that abort the request; safe sign-in redirect; contact form honeypot and keyed IP hash | No ceiling on total spend across accounts, with free sign-up and no email confirmation or captcha; the staff domain gets an admin seat on an unverified email; no output-token cap |
| **Reliability and tests** | 2 | Export guards (hidden tab, empty file, wrong length); storage quota handling; media fingerprint; about 700 unit tests plus about 139 e2e tests; rule-break testing | e2e never runs real sign-in, database or providers; the allowance's "can't pass the limit" guarantee is never run against Postgres; long exports are held in memory |
| **Deployment readiness and docs** | 1 | Boot-time setting checks; migrations deployed by CI with a drift check; row-level security checked; Sentry and Pino ready; known limits written down honestly | Transcripts and Make it requests exceed the intended host's request size; no function duration set; contact messages can't be read; several docs and copy lines don't match the code |

**How others might score it:**
- **A stricter reviewer: about 6.** On its intended host (Vercel, per `docs/rules/deployment.md`), the two headline features would fail for longer videos, and the main screen is one 2,560-line component. Many reviewers treat both as near-disqualifying, documented or not.
- **A kinder reviewer: about 10.** Nearly every weakness is already written down in ARCHITECTURE.md's known limits. The paid-action guards are better than most production apps. The test discipline (rule-break testing, replay fixtures, real-recording runs) is unusual for a project this size.

I haven't rounded up for effort.

## Findings, ranked

| # | Finding | Evidence (file:line) | Why it matters | Smallest fix | Size | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Transcripts and Make it don't fit the documented host.** A 5-minute transcript request is about 12.8 MB and a Make it plan about 9 MB, in one server-action body | `take-audio.ts:21-35`, `next.config.ts:30-33`; ARCHITECTURE.md:216-220 admits "Vercel's is 4.5 MB … these may fail there" | On Vercel, both features fail for anything longer than about 1.5–2 minutes. That's the core product | Pick a host without that cap and say so, or send audio to Deepgram from the browser with a short-lived, scoped key, and send fewer or smaller pictures | Medium–large | Sure about the sizes; the 4.5 MB figure is the repo's own citation |
| 2 | **No ceiling on total AI spend.** Limits are per account only, and accounts are free, instant, with no email confirmation and no captcha | `ai-limits.ts` (about $4.40 per account per day by its own cost notes), `ai-allowance.ts` (per user only), `supabase/config.toml:227`, `:214-217`, `login/actions.ts:40-56` | A script making accounts can spend without bound. It's a known limit (ARCHITECTURE.md:214) but has no mitigation | One global daily row per kind in `takeAllowance` (the same atomic update, across all users); turn on confirmations or a captcha in the hosted Supabase project | Small–medium | Sure |
| 3 | **Unverified emails can get a staff seat.** Signing up as `anyone@8x.social` creates a `read_only` admin seat, and email isn't confirmed | `staff-bootstrap.ts:9-11`, `provision-user.ts`, `supabase/config.toml:227` | Nothing reads with those permissions today (no admin pages, and flag actions need `flags.manage`), but the first admin page would open user data to anyone. `8x.social` is the template's domain, not Retake's | Empty `STAFF_DOMAIN_ROLE`, or require a confirmed email before seating | Small | Sure (latent) |
| 4 | **God component.** `VideoEditor` is one function with 53 `useState`, 15 `useRef` and 9 `useEffect` | `video-editor.tsx:236` to about `:2795` | Breaks the repo's own rule (`docs/rules/code-quality.md:35`); every editor change risks unrelated state; hard to review or test | Extract by concern: playback, project save, transcript jobs, export, panels state | Large | Sure |
| 5 | **Sign-in rate limits may be shared by all users.** Sign-in and sign-up call Supabase from the server, so Supabase's per-IP limit (30 per 5 minutes locally) sees the server's IP | `login/actions.ts:25-56`, `supabase/config.toml:208` | On a hosted project, one busy period could lock everyone out, or a password guesser shares everyone's budget | Check hosted Supabase behaviour; forward the client IP, or add an app-level per-IP limit | Small | Needs checking |
| 6 | **Contact messages can't be read.** The page promises a reply, but messages are only written | `contact/actions.ts:41` is the only use of `contactMessage` | Users write in and nobody sees it unless someone queries the database | A notification (email or the existing Slack webhook) on each message, or a small admin list | Small–medium | Sure |
| 7 | **No function duration set** for actions that can legitimately run 60 s | No `maxDuration` anywhere; `make-plan.ts:130` (`PLAN_TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000`) | Host defaults may cut the request before the app's own timeout, leaving the user an unexplained failure | Set `maxDuration` on the action routes to match the timeouts | Small | Needs checking (depends on host and plan) |
| 8 | **The Make it limit is misstated.** A plan, its check and each follow-up each cost one unit, while Pricing says "60 Make it requests a day" | `ai-limits.ts` (`make_it` comment); `messages/en.json:1642` | Users get about 30 plans a day, not 60 | Say "Make it steps", or don't charge the check separately | Small | Sure |
| 9 | **Copy promises music Make it can't add** | `messages/en.json:949, 1155, 1190, 1242`; `public/music/` has no tracks | It claims a feature that isn't there | My unfinished branch `fix/copy-no-make-it-music` does this | Small | Sure |
| 10 | **PostHog may capture transcript text.** Autocapture isn't turned off and replay isn't explicitly disabled | `instrumentation-client.ts:27-30` | Clicked transcript sentences are the user's spoken words, which conflicts with "Never your videos" on the Privacy page | Set `autocapture: false` (or mask the transcript and plan text) and disable session recording in code | Small | Needs checking (PostHog defaults and project settings) |
| 11 | **AI spend can't be traced to an account.** `ai_usage` has no user id | `prisma/schema/ai-usage.prisma` | You can see that money is being spent, but not who is spending it, which matters with finding 2 | Add a nullable `user_id`. The schema is a contract with the MOAD dashboard, so coordinate | Small–medium | Sure |
| 12 | **No output-token cap** on Gemini calls | No `maxOutputTokens` anywhere (`google.ts:74-81`) | A runaway reply costs more than the per-call estimates the limits assume | Set `maxOutputTokens` per feature (Make it used about 4,300 in the real run) | Small | Sure |
| 13 | **An error tells end users to edit `.env.local`**, and the docs say these failures are "generic" | `ai-busy.ts:23`; ARCHITECTURE.md:243 | It leaks setup details to users, and the docs are wrong about it | A generic message to users; the detail goes to the log | Small | Sure |
| 14 | **e2e tests never run the real server-action path.** CI has accounts off, so every paid action returns 401, and the helpers swap any `{"ok":false…}` for canned data | `ci.yml:92`; `e2e/make-helpers.ts` | A broken action, a wrong auth wiring or a bad schema would pass e2e. Unit tests (`paid-actions.test.mts`) cover the wrapper, but with the database and Supabase mocked | One CI job with local Supabase and Postgres, with fake providers at the network edge | Medium | Sure |
| 15 | **The allowance's atomicity is never tested on Postgres** | `paid-actions.test.mts:63` mocks `db` | The "can't pass the limit under parallel requests" claim (`ai-allowance.ts:7-9`) is unverified | A test against the CI `migrations` Postgres service that fires parallel takes | Small | Needs checking |
| 16 | **Every page view calls Supabase,** public pages included | `proxy.ts:105` matcher; `getUser()` at about `proxy.ts:55` | Adds latency and a network dependency to the landing page, and wastes auth calls | Skip the session refresh on public paths | Small | Sure |
| 17 | **About 1,640 lines of unused template scaffolding** (admin roles, flags, flag actions, route factory), and `example.prisma` holds the real `User` model | `src/lib/rbac`, `src/lib/flags`, `src/server/actions/feature-flags.ts`, `src/server/api` | Dead weight to read, keep secure (see finding 3) and keep green | Remove it, or fence it behind a clear "not used" note | Medium | Sure |
| 18 | **`make-plan.ts` is 2,058 lines:** reply schemas, planner steps, summaries and plan comparison together | `make-plan.ts` | Well tested, but slow to navigate and review | Split schemas, steps and summary into files; `planMake` itself is already short | Medium | Sure |
| 19 | **The contact limit can be passed by a burst:** it counts, then inserts | `contact/actions.ts:34-41` | A few extra messages in a burst; low impact | An insert with a conditional check, or a unique per-window counter | Small | Sure |
| 20 | **Docs don't match the code** | ARCHITECTURE.md:227 says about 1,880 lines (actually 2,058); `:230` says about 1,340 (about 1,640); `:243` says "generic error" (see finding 13); `DESIGN.md` radii (6/10/16 against 7/11/14/18/24/30 in `globals.css`) and component names (`app-screen.tsx` and others don't exist) | A reviewer checking claims finds them off | Correct the numbers, or link to the files instead of quoting sizes | Small | Sure |
| 21 | **Migrations and the app deploy aren't ordered** | `deploy-migrations.yml:3-5` runs on push, independently of the host's deploy | New code can briefly run against the old schema | Make the deploy wait for the migration job, or use expand-then-contract migrations | Small–medium | Needs checking |

## Fine, or deliberate trade-offs to keep documented

- **The paid-action wrapper** (`validated-action.ts`): it signs in first and fails closed, validates before spending, takes the allowance before calling the provider, and gives it back on failure. Errors pass through `captureError`, so raw errors never reach users. This is good design.
- **Timeouts abort the provider request** (`google.ts:74-81`, `deepgram.ts:66-79`), and every call is recorded with its cost; unpriced calls are recorded as such, not as $0.
- **The AI chokepoint** is enforced by ESLint for the Gemini SDK (`eslint.config.mjs:32-33, 151`). Deepgram uses plain `fetch`, so a new raw call elsewhere wouldn't be caught. Worth a sentence in the docs.
- **The sign-in redirect** (`login/next-path.ts`) rejects `//` and backslash tricks and other origins. It's correct.
- **The contact form:** a honeypot, a keyed IP hash, one refusal message for every reason, and a required production secret. It's reasonable, apart from findings 6 and 19.
- **Boot-time checks** for missing production settings (`production-settings.ts`, `instrumentation.ts`), and `env.ts` validation.
- **Browser-side export:** in real time, with guards for a hidden tab, an empty file and the wrong length (`export-button.tsx:125-175`). It's a sound trade-off (no server media cost). The cost is that the tab must stay open and everything is held in memory; it's documented.
- **IndexedDB projects:** quota is handled, saves complete or fail, persistent storage is requested (`projects.ts:291`), and media is matched by name, size, modified time and a 2 MB SHA-256 fingerprint. Projects being per-browser is a documented trade-off.
- **Migrations by CI** with a drift check and row-level security checks; the honest Known limits section.

## Could this be deployed for real users?

**Not as is on Vercel. Yes on a host without the 4.5 MB request cap, after a few small changes:**
1. a total-spend ceiling, plus confirmations or a captcha (finding 2);
2. an empty staff-domain map (finding 3);
3. contact messages someone actually receives (finding 6);
4. `maxDuration` set (finding 7);
5. PostHog autocapture switched off (finding 10).

Until then, cost exposure is open-ended and the two main AI features fail for longer videos on the documented host.

## Recommendation

**Do first:**
1. **Cap total spend.** Add a global daily ceiling in `takeAllowance`, and turn on email confirmation or a captcha in the hosted Supabase project. It's small, and it removes the one unbounded-cost risk.
2. **Decide the host and make the big requests fit it.** Either a host without the body cap (documented, with `maxDuration` set), or browser-direct transcription with a short-lived Deepgram key. Without this, transcripts and Make it fail on Vercel.
3. **Close the latent admin hole and the privacy gap:** empty `STAFF_DOMAIN_ROLE`, and set `autocapture: false` with session recording disabled. Both are one-line changes.

**Leave for later:** splitting `VideoEditor` and `make-plan.ts`, removing the unused scaffolding, and the real-integration CI job. They're worth doing, but they don't block a careful launch.

**Not checked:**
- hosted Supabase or PostHog settings;
- Vercel's current limits beyond the repo's own citation;
- phone layouts;
- performance under load;
- the full e2e suite (not run for this review).

## PR 2 findings, checked against the code

1. **Staff seat from an email domain: true.** `src/lib/rbac/staff-bootstrap.ts:10` gives any `@8x.social` email the lowest admin role on first sign-in. Email confirmation is off, so anyone can sign up with an address at that domain and get the seat.
2. **No output-token cap: true.** None of the five Gemini calls sets `maxOutputTokens`.
3. **PostHog: true.** `posthog.init` in `instrumentation-client.ts` doesn't set `autocapture` (so it's on by default) or `disable_session_recording`. Whether sessions are recorded is decided in PostHog's own settings, not in code. Page views are captured by hand, and I'll keep that.
4. **Contact limit not atomic: true.** `contact/actions.ts:34-43` counts recent messages, then inserts, as two separate queries. Parallel requests can all pass the count.
   **"No local way to read the messages": partly wrong.** The schema comment says to use Prisma Studio or the `sql-preview` skill, and both work. What's missing is a quick, purpose-built way to read them.
5. **Supabase call on public pages: true.** `src/proxy.ts` calls `supabase.auth.getUser()` on every page request, but only uses the result to guard `/home`, `/make`, `/projects`, `/editor` and `/login`. The public pages never read the session, so skipping the call there loses nothing.
6. **No lint rule for Deepgram: true.** Deepgram is a plain `fetch` in `src/server/ai/deepgram.ts`, and the ESLint rule only blocks imports of `@google/genai`. A `fetch` to `api.deepgram.com` anywhere else would pass lint.

## Question 1: output-token caps

The numbers below are output tokens per call from the local `ai_usage` table: 360 successful real Gemini calls, 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-12. I didn't make any AI call to get them.

| Feature | Calls | Median | 95th percentile | Largest | Proposed cap | Headroom over largest |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `parse_edit_command` (typed command) | 215 | 110 | 152 | 161 | **1,024** | 6× |
| `find_highlights` | 11 | 66 | 139 | 169 | **1,024** | 6× |
| `make_it_check` | 33 | 126 | 196 | 211 | **1,024** | 4.9× |
| `make_it` (the plan) | 44 | 464 | 4,202 | 5,483 | **8,192** | 1.5× |
| `make_it_follow_up` | 2 | 239 | 239 | 239 | **8,192** | 34× |

How I got the caps:
- **Why headroom matters:** if a reply hits the cap, its JSON is cut off and fails to parse. The person then sees "couldn't plan" or "unsupported", and the call is still paid for.
- **Small replies:** typed commands, highlights and the check get a round 1,024, comfortably above anything seen.
- **The plan:** its largest replies come from 4–5 minute recordings, because the reply lists a face position for each picture (up to 150). 8,192 is 1.5× the largest real reply.
- **The follow-up:** it returns the same reply format as a plan, so it gets the plan's cap.
- **Not counted:** four `make_it` rows from 2026-10-06 (output exactly 100, input exactly 1,000) look like test rows. Only the 2 follow-up samples exist, which is thin; that's another reason to give it the plan's cap.

I'd also log a reply cut off by the cap as its own event, so it's never mistaken for a malformed reply.

**Do you approve these five caps (1,024 / 1,024 / 1,024 / 8,192 / 8,192)?** My recommendation is yes, as shown. Once you've answered, I'll ask the contact-messages question, with my recommendation.

**Done on `fix/hardening` (uncommitted):**
- **Output caps:**
  - All five caps live in one constant, `GEMINI_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`, in `src/server/ai/google.ts` next to the model setting. The wrapper applies the cap for the call's feature, and a cap a caller passes can't replace it.
  - A Gemini call for a feature with no cap doesn't compile.
  - A reply cut off by its cap is logged as its own event (`AI reply cut off by its output cap`, with the feature and the cap).
  - The comment says 8,192 is 1.5× the largest plan seen, not a proven bound. It names bounding the reply's schema and setting a thinking budget as the known next step.
  - New tests cover:
    - every Gemini call has a cap;
    - each action sends its own cap;
    - a cut-off plan is logged with `make_it` and comes back as no plan, not a crash;
    - in a new e2e test, the page then shows "Retake couldn't make a plan from that. Try saying it another way."
- **PostHog:** autocapture and session recording are now off in code; page views are still captured by hand. The init settings are in `src/lib/posthog/init-options.ts`, with a test. The feature-flags doc now says recording is off in code.
- **Proxy:** it calls Supabase only on `/home`, `/make`, `/projects`, `/editor`, `/login` and `/admin`. A new test checks that public pages make no Supabase call and that the sign-in redirects are unchanged.
- **Deepgram lint rule:** any `deepgram.com` string outside `src/server/ai/` now fails lint. A new test runs the repo's own lint config on sample code.
- **Contact limit:**
  - The count and the insert now run in one Serializable transaction. If two sends overlap, Postgres aborts one and it's tried again, up to 3 times.
  - One change you didn't ask for: a send still overlapping after 3 tries is refused with the usual "couldn't be sent right now" message (429), instead of a server error. Without that, a burst of sends from one person showed up as errors in Sentry.
  - Five new tests, with a fake database that behaves like Postgres here. Real Postgres isn't covered yet; I'd add that to PR 3 alongside the allowance test.
- **`pnpm contact:messages`** follows your conditions:
  - it runs one read-only select, printing the latest 20 by default (`--limit`, at most 200);
  - it never prints the hashed IP;
  - it refuses any database that isn't on this machine.

  Locally it printed the 3 QA test messages; pointed at a remote URL it refused. Four tests cover formatting and that it only selects. The README and the "personal data stays local" note are still to write.
- **Staff seat:** the domain map is now empty, and its comment, `provision-user.ts` and `docs/rules/authorization.md` are updated.

**The five existing tests that need to change:**

1. **`src/lib/rbac/staff-bootstrap.test.mts`, two tests.** Both asserted that an `8x.social` email gets a seat, which is the behaviour this PR removes. I'd keep both tests and assert the opposite:

| Old | New |
|---|---|
| `test('8x.social emails get the lowest admin seat', () => {`<br>`  assert.equal(seatRoleForEmail('lumin@8x.social'), LOWEST_ROLE)`<br>`})` | `test('8x.social emails get no admin seat: every seat is granted by hand', () => {`<br>`  assert.equal(seatRoleForEmail('lumin@8x.social'), null)`<br>`})` |
| `test('the domain match is case-insensitive', () => {`<br>`  assert.equal(seatRoleForEmail('Someone@8X.Social'), LOWEST_ROLE)`<br>`})` | `test('an 8x.social email in any case gets no seat', () => {`<br>`  assert.equal(seatRoleForEmail('Someone@8X.Social'), null)`<br>`})` |

   The `LOWEST_ROLE` import would go, since nothing uses it.

2. **`src/app/[locale]/(site)/contact/actions.test.mts`, its fake database.** The action now calls `db.$transaction`, which the fake doesn't have, so three of its four tests fail on a TypeError. I'd add one method to the fake. None of the tests or assertions change:

| Old (the `db` fake) | New |
|---|---|
| `db: {`<br>`  contactMessage: { count…, create… }`<br>`}` | `db: {`<br>`  contactMessage: { count…, create… },`<br>`  $transaction: (body) => body({ contactMessage: <the same object> })`<br>`}` |

   (The `contactMessage` object would be declared once and used in both places.)

The database is reachable now. The container was cleanly restarted at 11:31:52 UTC, and a read-only `select 1` just succeeded. I didn't start, stop, restart, delete or reset anything.

**1. How the repo starts local Supabase**
- The tool is the Supabase CLI, run through `pnpm dlx`. That downloads and runs it on the spot, so nothing is installed. It isn't a dependency in `package.json`, which is why `supabase` and `pnpm exec supabase` aren't found.
- From the repo root (README "Run it locally", and `.claude/skills/dev-onboarding/SKILL.md`):
  ```
  pnpm dlx supabase start     # Postgres and Auth in Docker (first run downloads ~2 GB)
  pnpm dlx supabase status    # prints the local URLs and keys
  pnpm exec prisma migrate deploy
  ```
- It needs Docker Desktop running (on Windows, with WSL2).
- `supabase/config.toml` sets `project_id = "t1-alishbasaboor"`, the API on 54321 and the database on 54322. Nothing under `docs/` covers starting Supabase.

**2. `docker ps -a`**

| Container | Status | Ports |
|---|---|---|
| `supabase_db_t1-alishbasaboor` | Up 6 minutes (healthy) | `0.0.0.0:54322->5432/tcp`, `[::]:54322->5432/tcp` |
| `supabase_kong_t1-alishbasaboor` | Up 54 minutes (healthy) | `0.0.0.0:54321->8000/tcp` |
| `supabase_auth_t1-alishbasaboor` | Up 54 minutes (healthy) | `9999/tcp` (not published) |
| `supabase_inbucket_t1-alishbasaboor` | Up 54 minutes (healthy) | `0.0.0.0:54324->8025/tcp` |

- **The database container is running, not exited.** Its restart count is 0 and its restart policy is `unless-stopped`.
- **Its log shows a clean stop at 11:31:50 and a start two seconds later**, so it was stopped and started rather than crashing. On start it printed "Database directory appears to contain a database; Skipping initialization", so the data was kept. It has accepted connections since 11:31:52.
- **That restart explains my failed attempts.** The other containers were up while I was getting connection refusals.

**3. If it stops answering again, run these in order, from the repo root**
1. Make sure Docker Desktop is open and shows "Engine running".
2. `docker ps -a`, and look at `supabase_db_t1-alishbasaboor`.
3. If it shows `Exited`: `docker start supabase_db_t1-alishbasaboor`. This starts the existing container with its existing volume, so no data is lost.
4. If several containers are down, or step 3 isn't enough: `pnpm dlx supabase start`. This starts the existing containers and volumes and doesn't reinitialise a database that already has data, so no data is lost.
5. `pnpm dlx supabase status` should list the DB URL on port 54322.
6. If the container is up and healthy but connections are still refused, the port forwarding is stuck. Quit and reopen Docker Desktop (tray icon → Quit Docker Desktop, then start it again), then repeat steps 2–5. This doesn't touch volumes, so no data is lost.

Never use `supabase db reset`, `supabase stop --no-backup`, `docker compose down -v`, `docker volume prune` or `docker rm -v`. Those can delete the data.

Since it answers now, I can run `pnpm test:db` locally whenever you say. Nothing is committed yet.

The README draft is in the scratchpad (`README.draft.md`). The repo file is untouched, and no branch exists yet. Here it is in full:

````markdown
# Retake

An AI video editor for fixing and polishing recordings you already have.
Load a video, and Retake helps you clean it up, edit it by hand, or turn it
into something new, without shooting it again.

## What it does

**Make it with AI.** Turns a recording into a reel, an ad or a TikTok.

- Type what you want, answer a few quick questions, and approve the plan before anything changes.
- Pick one of four looks: Clean, Bold, Cinematic or Playful.

**Manual editing.** A timeline editor.

- Trim, split, merge and reorder clips.
- Add captions, titles, a logo, zoom, colour, speed changes and transitions.
- An audio lane, a before/after comparison, and export to a video file.

**Automatic cleanup and re-recording.**

- A transcript, with silences and filler words found for you.
- One-click cleanup, or cut fillers one at a time.
- Re-record a single sentence and splice it back in.

## How it works

Your video and your projects stay in the browser: editing, preview and export
run there, and saved projects are kept in the browser's storage (IndexedDB).
For the AI features only, a video's audio goes to Deepgram (transcripts and
spoken commands), and Gemini receives text (typed commands and the
transcript) and, for Make it, small still frames taken every 2 seconds. The
video file itself is never uploaded. Sign-in runs on a local Supabase in
Docker. The server also downloads AI model prices from OpenRouter, which
receives none of your data. Sentry and PostHog send nothing unless their keys
are set. Details are on the Privacy page, at `/privacy`
([source](<src/app/[locale]/(site)/privacy/page.tsx>)).

## Stack

- Next.js 16 and React 19, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS 4, next-intl
- Postgres through Prisma
- Supabase Auth, run locally in Docker
- Gemini and Deepgram for the AI features
- Browser-side export, with projects saved in IndexedDB
- pnpm, Playwright, GitHub Actions

## Folder structure

```
.github/        CI workflows
docs/           engineering rules and CI/CD setup
e2e/            Playwright end-to-end tests
messages/       UI text (en.json)
prisma/         database schema and migrations
public/         static files, including Make it's music
scripts/        repo checks and database helpers
supabase/       local Supabase config
src/
  app/          pages: landing, privacy, sign-in, home, projects, Make it, the editor
  components/   shared UI components
  i18n/         locale routing
  lib/          shared code: env, errors, analytics, flags, Supabase helpers
  server/       server actions, AI provider wrappers, database client
```

## Run it locally

You need:

- Node 22.18 or later, and pnpm
- Docker Desktop (for local Supabase)
- Optional: a Gemini API key and a Deepgram API key

Then, from the repo root:

```bash
pnpm install                      # also generates the Prisma client
pnpm dlx supabase start           # Postgres and Auth in Docker
pnpm dlx supabase status          # prints the local URLs and keys
cp .env.example .env.local        # then fill it in (below)
pnpm exec prisma migrate deploy   # create the tables
pnpm dev                          # http://localhost:3000
```

In `.env.local`, set these from `pnpm dlx supabase status`: `DATABASE_URL`
and `DIRECT_URL` (the local Postgres), `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and
`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`. Create an account on the sign-in page
to use the app.

The AI keys go in `.env.local` too:

- `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY`: transcripts and spoken commands. Cleanup,
  re-recording, highlights and Make it all start from the transcript.
- `GEMINI_API_KEY`: typed commands, highlights and Make it's plan.

Without them the app still runs, and the editor and manual editing work;
only the AI features fail, with an error message.

## Tests

```bash
pnpm test        # unit tests (node --test)
pnpm test:e2e    # Playwright end-to-end tests; starts the app unless E2E_BASE_URL is set
```

No test calls a real AI provider.

## Architecture

Decisions, trade-offs, scope and known limits are in
[ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md).

## Scope

Retake is local-first: it runs on your own computer and is not deployed.
````

Where the draft differs from your brief, and why:
- **No database-tests line.** `pnpm test:db` isn't on main; it's only in the unmerged PR 3 branch.
- **No quoted error message.** The old README said a missing AI key shows "The AI isn't available right now." In main's code, that message is only for a key the provider *refuses*. An unset key fails as a generic error, so the draft says only "fail, with an error message".
- **Both keys for Make it and highlights.** Both read the Deepgram transcript, so they need both keys, and the key lines say so.
- **Every command and path in the draft comes from main's `package.json`, `git ls-tree` and the source.** I'll run the read-only command checks and list how I checked each one once you approve. Running them now would load the machine while the PR 3 e2e suite is still going on :3200.
- **The branch comes after PR 3.** My working tree still holds PR 3's uncommitted work, so I'll create the README branch from main only after PR 3 is committed and pushed.

**One question:** your brief says audio goes to Deepgram and still frames to Gemini, and "nothing else leaves the computer". Main's code and Privacy page show three more things:
- Gemini also gets text: typed commands and the transcript.
- The server downloads AI prices from OpenRouter, with none of your data.
- Sentry and PostHog send data if their keys are set.

**I recommend keeping the draft's fuller paragraph, which states all three**, because "nothing else" wouldn't be true. Is that wording OK, or do you want it shorter?
